ENGINEERED FOR GROWTH. 
POWERED BY PURPOSE.
Engineered for Growth. Powered by 
Purpose  reflects Premier Energies’ 
belief that enduring growth is achieved when engineering excellence is guided by a clear sense of purpose.
The Company has built its business through  
careful planning, disciplined execution and a relentless focus on engineering excellence. Every investment in manufacturing capacity, integrated operations, advanced technologies and human capital has been thoughtfully engineered to strengthen competitiveness, enhance resilience and create long-term growth. As Premier Energies evolves into an integrated clean energy solutions company, this engineering-led approach continues to shape  its strategy and future direction.As India accelerates its transition towards a cleaner and more sustainable energy future, Premier Energies remains committed to enabling this transformation through responsible manufacturing, technological innovation and sustainable business practices. The Company’s purpose extends beyond delivering high-quality clean energy solutions to creating value for customers, empowering employees, supporting communities and generating sustainable returns for shareholders.
Engineered for Growth. Powered by 
Purpose embodies this dual commitment. 
It represents an organisation that is building 
scale with discipline and pursuing growth that is responsible and meaningful for every stakeholder.

=== page 4 ===
We deliver integrated 
solar manufacturing solutions, supported by expanding capabilities in adjacent clean energy technologies.Established in 1995, we are one of India’s leading integrated solar manufacturing companies, with three decades of experience in photovoltaic technology. From our humble beginnings in module assembly, we have evolved into a scaled, technology-led manufacturing organisation with capabilities spanning solar cells and modules, supported by growing backward integration and investments across the clean energy value chain.
WHO WE ARE
WHAT WE DO
An Integrated Cleantech EcosystemOur manufacturing operations are built around scale, 
automation and quality, with a strong emphasis on adopting next-generation technologies. We were among the first Indian manufacturers to produce TOPCon cells and continue to invest in advanced manufacturing capabilities to enhance efficiency, reliability and performance. Our facilities and products meet leading domestic and international standards, reflecting our commitment to manufacturing excellence.
We manufacture high-efficiency solar cells and modules, supported by an increasingly integrated manufacturing base that extends upstream into ingots and wafers. Alongside these core operations, we are developing capabilities in battery energy storage systems, transformers and aluminium frames to create a broader clean technology portfolio capable of supporting the evolving requirements of our customers.
Our products serve a diverse customer base 
comprising utility-scale developers, independent power producers, EPC contractors, commercial and industrial enterprises, residential customers and farmers in India and select international markets. 
Through manufacturing excellence, technology 
leadership and a strong commitment to quality, we deliver solutions that support India’s clean energy transition.

OUR MARKET OFFERINGS - END-TO-END SOLUTIONS ACROSS PV AND EMERGING CLEANTECH
The Company offers a comprehensive portfolio of solar photovoltaic products and emerging clean energy solutions 
designed to meet the diverse requirements of utility-scale, commercial & industrial, residential and government customers. Leveraging advanced manufacturing, technology innovation and an integrated value chain, the Company is steadily expanding its offerings to support the evolving needs of the renewable energy eco-system.
High-efficiency Mono PERC and TOPCon solar cells manufactured using automated production lines designed to deliver conversion efficiencies of up to 25.5%, consistent quality and long-term reliability
Aluminium frame manufacturing to enhance backward integration and improve supply chain controlA comprehensive portfolio of Mono PERC and TOPCon solar modules designed for utility-scale, commercial & industrial and rooftop applications. Manufactured on highly automated production lines, the modules support bifacial configurations and comply with applicable domestic and international quality standards. Manufacturing excellence
Manufacturing lies at the heart of the Company’s business model. Over three decades, 
we have built a robust manufacturing ecosystem founded on engineering precision, process discipline and a relentless focus on quality. Every stage of production is designed to deliver consistency, reliability and operational efficiency, enabling the Company to meet the evolving expectations of customers in India and global markets.
Process excellence, continuous improvement and technology adoption remain central to ensuring reliable products, operational efficiency and scalable growth. Through continuous investments in manufacturing infrastructure, engineering expertise and integrated capabilities, we are building an agile organisation that can support future capacity expansion, product innovation and respond effectively to changing customer requirements.

Powering Progress Through PeopleIn FY 2026, we conducted 16,160 training and awareness 
hours across the company, reinforcing our commitment to continuous learning, awareness, and capability building across the organisation.
At the end of the year, our workforce comprised 9,080 
people, with women representing 29.2% of the total workforce. Our workforce mix reflects the scale of our manufacturing operations as well as our continued focus on building a diverse talent base.
CREATING A CULTURE OF CONNECTION AND 
RECOGNITION
We aim to create a vibrant, inclusive and employee-centric 
workplace where people feel connected to the organisation and to one another. Employee engagement initiatives span culture, wellness, sports and recognition, creating opportunities for employees to participate beyond their day-to-day roles.
Festivals, Women’s Day initiatives and Annual Day 
celebrations, strengthen employee connect and foster a sense of belonging. Yoga Day, comprehensive health and eye check-ups, and annual sports activities support employee wellness and encourage healthier lifestyles. Structured rewards and recognition initiatives acknowledge people’s contributions, achievements and performance, reinforcing a culture of appreciation and accountability.
This commitment to our people has been recognised 
through our Great Place to Work® certification for five consecutive years, reflecting our sustained focus on Behind every manufacturing milestone, technological advancement and customer partnership is a team united by a shared commitment to excellence. As Premier Energies scales its manufacturing footprint and capabilities, we remain focused on building a  high-performing organisation that attracts, develops and retains talent across all corporate functions.
We believe sustainable growth begins with an environment where people are empowered to learn, collaborate and contribute. Our people practices are designed to strengthen technical capabilities, nurture leadership, encourage innovation and create opportunities for our people to grow alongside the organisation.

SAFETY AS A FUNDAMENTAL RESPONSIBILITY
Safety remains a non-negotiable priority across our manufacturing and operational facilities. We continue to strengthen safety awareness, systems and practices with the objective of creating a workplace where every employee can perform their responsibilities safely and confidently.
Our FY 2026 safety performance reflects this focus. Zero 
work-related fatalities were reported during the year. We recorded one Lost Time Injury (LTI) during the year, with the last reportable accident recorded on 3 September 2025. Medical Treatment Cases (MTC) stood at 12, well below the threshold of 45, while the Incident Frequency Rate was 0.048, indicating a low occurrence of incidents relative to workforce exposure.ADVANCING INCLUSION AND WOMEN’S 
PARTICIPATION
We are committed to creating a safe, inclusive and 
supportive workplace where women are empowered to participate, contribute and grow across the organisation.
Our diversity initiatives address women’s needs across 
different stages of their professional journey. During pregnancy and motherhood, we provide supportive measures including flexible work arrangements, additional breaks, transportation assistance and workplace care measures. These initiatives are intended to provide women employees with greater support during an important phase of their lives while enabling them to remain connected to the workplace.
We also continue to strengthen women’s safety and 
workplace inclusion through POSH initiatives, safe transportation and other supportive measures. Focused hiring and employment opportunities are helping increase participation of women on the shopfloor, supporting a more diverse workforce across our operations.

=== page 13 ===
GROWTH DRIVERS
Macro Trends Accelerating Long-
Term Opportunities
The global energy transition is reshaping the renewable energy landscape, supportive 
policy frameworks and evolving customer requirements. These structural drivers are accelerating investments across the solar value chain and creating significant opportunities for future growth of the business.
FOCUS ON NATIONAL  
ENERGY SECURITY
Energy security has become a strategic priority for 
countries worldwide. Reducing reliance on imported fossil fuels, strengthening domestic manufacturing capabilities and building resilient supply chains are driving sustained investments in renewable energy infrastructure. In India, this has translated into strong policy support for domestic solar manufacturing and localisation across the photovoltaic value chain.STRONG DEMAND MOMENTUM
India continues to witness robust demand across utility-scale projects, commercial & industrial installations, rooftop solar and government-led programmes. Rising electricity consumption, corporate sustainability commitments and expanding renewable energy deployment are supporting long-term demand for high-quality solar products and integrated clean energy solutions.POLICY SUPPORT
Government initiatives including domestic manufacturing incentives, localisation measures and renewable energy programmes continue to strengthen India’s clean energy manufacturing ecosystem.INTERNATIONAL MARKET OPPORTUNITIES
The global diversification of solar supply chains is creating new opportunities for Indian manufacturers with integrated capabilities and globally competitive products. Increasing demand for trusted manufacturing partners, coupled with the growing preference for diversified sourcing, is expanding export opportunities across international markets.
EVOLUTION OF THE SOLAR  
MANUFACTURING ECOSYSTEM
The photovoltaic industry is evolving from standalone 
manufacturing towards integrated value chains that encompass upstream materials, cell manufacturing and module assembly. This transformation is being driven by the need for supply chain resilience, improved quality assurance, operational efficiencies and enhanced global competitiveness, particularly as countries seek to localise clean energy manufacturing.EMERGING OPPORTUNITIES BEYOND SOLAR
The rapid expansion of renewable energy is increasing demand for complementary technologies, including battery energy storage systems, grid infrastructure and transformers. As customers seek integrated clean energy solutions, manufacturers with diversified capabilities are well positioned to participate in the next phase of industry growth.

Competitive Strengths
ENGINEERED TO COMPETE.  
POSITIONED TO LEAD.
We have built our competitive position through decades 
of manufacturing experience, continuous technology adoption and disciplined capacity expansion. As the renewable energy landscape evolves, the Company continues to strengthen the capabilities that enable it to create sustainable value across market cycles.
MANUFACTURING SCALE WITH  
OPERATIONAL EXCELLENCE
Over three decades, the Company has developed 
advanced manufacturing capabilities supported by automation, precision engineering and robust quality systems. Continuous investments in manufacturing infrastructure have enabled us to expand capacity while maintaining consistent product quality and operational efficiency. This strong manufacturing foundation allows the Company to serve a diverse customer base across utility-scale, commercial and industrial, rooftop and government-supported projects. TECHNOLOGY AND INNOVATION LED 
Technological leadership is driven by combining breakthrough R&D, strategic partnerships, and advanced digital manufacturing. The Company developed India’s first Zero Busbar (0BB) TOPCon solar cell and introduced the premium NeoBlack™ all-black TOPCon module series for high-performance rooftop applications. To accelerate cell efficiency innovations, we collaborate with global technology leaders like RENA Technology GmbH and academic institutions like BITS Hyderabad, resulting in multiple peer-reviewed research publications. The manufacturing ecosystem is powered by AI-driven automated guided vehicles (AGVs), natural-language live dashboards, and an IoT framework integrating Manufacturing Execution System, Building Management System, and Energy Management System. This digital infrastructure delivers real-time asset traceability, automated power quality optimization, and predictive maintenance, ensuring high manufacturing excellence, lower operating costs, and superior product quality.DIVERSIFIED CUSTOMER RELATIONSHIPS
The Company serves customers across multiple segments, including utility-scale developers, independent power producers, EPC companies, commercial and industrial enterprises, exports and government programmes. This diversified customer base provides access to multiple growth opportunities while reducing dependence on any single market segment. 
QUALITY FOCUS
The Company is the only Indian solar manufacturer to operate a LEED Gold-certified facility, reflecting our commitment to environmentally responsible operations. Our modules have been recognised as Top Performers on the Kiwa reliability scorecard for three consecutive years. These prestigious certifications strengthen our brand credibility and enhance competitiveness in the global marketAN INTEGRATED  
MANUFACTURING PLATFORM
The Company has progressively expanded from 
manufacturing solar modules to building an integrated presence across the photovoltaic value chain. With solar cell and module manufacturing at its core, the Company is strengthening backward integration through investments in ingots and wafers while expanding into complementary businesses such as battery energy storage systems, solar inverters, transformers and aluminium frames. This integrated approach enhances supply chain resilience, improves operational efficiencies and creates opportunities for diversified growth.

=== page 15 ===
Scaling Integration, Production 
Capacities & ExecutionChairman & Managing Director
Dear Shareholders, 
The energy transition phenomenon has entered a new 
phase. The focus is shifting from merely deploying renewable energy to delivering firm power solutions, advanced manufacturing capabilities, and resilient supply chains. What was once driven primarily by climate ambition is now equally shaped by energy security, industrial competitiveness and the immediate need for self-reliance. As clean energy infrastructure and domestic manufacturing scale up, long-term leadership will increasingly be determined by innovation, vertical integration, and consistent execution. 
India, backed by ambitious renewable targets and a 
supportive policy framework, is exceptionally well-placed to lead this global transformation. Premier Energies has positioned itself to capture this moment by consistently investing ahead of demand and expanding its strengths across the clean energy value chain. For over three decades, our evolution has been guided by a clear belief: enduring competitiveness comes from building in-house technology. We began as a solar module manufacturer, expanded into solar cells, and progressively developed one of India’s largest integrated photovoltaic platforms. Today, we are taking the next decisive leap. We are extending our footprint through upstream integration into ingots and wafers, while expanding into Battery Energy Storage Systems (BESS) and transformers. This is a natural progression of our business model - enabling us to serve a wider spectrum of customer needs while creating compounding value across the renewable ecosystem. 
Record Performance Driven by  
Disciplined Execution
FY26 marked another landmark milestone in our journey, 
defined by record top-line expansion, enhanced margins, and sustained capital efficiency. Revenue from operations surged to ₹ 8,026 Crore, representing a robust 20.7%  year-on-year increase. EBITDA grew by 35% year-on-year to ₹ 2,579 Crore. Bottom-line growth was equally strong as Profit After Tax expanded 61.1% year-on-year to ₹ 1,510 Crore. Throughout this period, we maintained exceptional capital discipline, yielding a Return on Capital Employed of 33.5%, a Return on Equity of 42.4%, and a Net Debt to EBITDA ratio of 0.41x. We closed the year with a robust order book standing at ₹ 14,010 Crore, representing 9,383 MW of production volume visibility.
The strength of our order book reflects more than 
favourable market conditions; it signifies the deep confidence our customers place in our execution capability, product quality, and ability to deliver at scale. Scaling Capabilities Across the Ecosystem
These financial achievements are the natural outcome of a long-term strategy that prioritises capability creation over short-term gains. Over the past year, we consciously expanded our integrated manufacturing ecosystem to fortify our competitiveness across the entire PV value chain. During FY26, our installed platform expanded to 11.1 GW of solar module capacity, following the commissioning of our state-of-the-art 5.6 GW facility and 3.6 GW solar cell capacity. We produced 3,570 MW modules and 2,268 MW cells, maintaining excellent capacity utilisation rates. 
Construction at our Naidupeta complex in Andhra Pradesh 
is progressing on schedule, with 10 GW of planned ingot and wafer capacity. We are aggressively scaling adjacent cleantech capabilities to capture emerging market demand and build a comprehensive ecosystem. In energy storage, we are progressing toward a 12 GWh containerised battery storage facility planned by 2028 to address surging grid-scale storage requirements. Simultaneously, we are  establishing an 18,000 MTPA aluminium frame facility for captive consumption. 
Complementing these organic investments for captive 
consumption we completed 51% acquisition of Transcon Industries. This acquisition takes our operational transformer capacity to 6.75 GVA, with an upcoming  10 GVA Extra High Voltage facility (up to 765 kV) targeted for completion in Q3 2026 to further fortify our integrated growth trajectory.
Diversifying into BESS and specialised grid equipment is 
expected to contribute 25% of consolidated revenues by 2030. Our integrated clean energy solutions strengthen long-term partnerships with leading EPC contractors and Independent Power Producers, positioning us as an indispensable end-to-end solutions partner.
Technology, Quality, and Manufacturing 
Excellence
Innovation remains central to our leadership. As 
photovoltaic technology advances rapidly, continuous product development and process refinement are essential. During the year, we accelerated our transition toward advanced N-Type TOPCon technology while enriching our portfolio with Zero Busbar solar cells and high-efficiency all-black modules.
Concurrently, investments in plant automation, digital 
manufacturing, and AI-enabled quality controls have elevated process precision and yield consistency. The real-world quality of our engineering continues to receive global validation: Our modules were recognised as Top Performers in the Kiwa PVEL Module Reliability Scorecard for the third consecutive year.Empowering People, Embedding ESG, and 
Strengthening Governance
None of these milestones would be possible without 
our people. We continue to invest in technical expertise, leadership development, and a culture anchored in ownership and continuous improvement. Our team’s dedication has allowed us to scale rapidly without sacrificing agility. Our continued recognition as a Great Place to Work® is a testament to the collaborative, high-accountability environment our workforce has built. Over 30% of our plant workforce is female, and our operations currently support over 9,200 green jobs. 
The same philosophy underpins our approach to 
sustainability and governance. ESG is embedded in our operational core. In FY26 alone, our solar manufacturing output helped avoid an estimated 100 MMT CO2e emissions. During FY26, we published our Inaugural Sustainability Report, completed a double materiality assessment, mapped our GHG inventory, and formalised our decarbonization roadmap aligned with the UN SDGs. 
As our enterprise grows, effective oversight and transparent 
decision-making become even more vital. Supported by an independent board and robust internal controls, we remain committed to the highest standards of corporate ethics, compliance, and governance. 
Looking Ahead 
Growing power demand, increasing adoption of energy storage, advancements in power electronics, grid modernisation, and localised manufacturing are reshaping the global clean energy landscape. Companies that anticipate these structural shifts and invest ahead of the curve will be the ones to build enduring enterprise value.
By deepening upstream integration, broadening our 
cleantech portfolio, and maintaining rigorous financial discipline, the Company is preparing for its next phase of growth. We enter the new financial year with high visibility, strong operational tailwinds, and a clear strategic roadmap. 
We extend heartfelt gratitude to our shareholders, 
customers, employees, partners and suppliers for their unwavering trust. Together, we are manufacturing the future of clean energy. 
Warm regards
Surenderpal Singh Saluja
Chairman
Chiranjeev Singh Saluja
Managing Director

Building a 
Sustainable Future Through Planet, People, and ProductESG Framework 
Premier Energies has evolved its ESG approach from 
a compliance-driven function into a strategic business priority, integrating sustainability into governance, operations and long-term decision-making. Guided by its double materiality assessment, the Company has structured its ESG framework around three strategic pillars: Planet Positive, People Centric and Product Leadership. Together, these pillars reflect its commitment to responsible growth, long-term stakeholder value and India’s clean energy transition.REDUCING OUR ENVIRONMENTAL FOOTPRINT THROUGH RESPONSIBLE OPERATIONS
As a renewable energy manufacturer we are focused on improving energy efficiency, increasing the use of renewable energy, conserving water, reducing emissions and strengthening circularity across its operations.
The Company continues to advance energy optimisation, renewable energy adoption and emissions reduction initiatives 
as part of its decarbonisation journey.
These initiatives reflect our focus on responsible resource management, water conservation and greater recovery and 
reuse of materials across its operations.ENERGY & CLIMATE

BUILDING CAPABILITIES, STRENGTHENING SAFETY AND CREATING AN INCLUSIVE WORKPLACE
Our people are central to our ability to scale responsibly and sustain operational excellence. During FY2026, the 
Company continued to strengthen its workforce, invest in capability development and embed health, safety and inclusion across the organisation.
STRENGTHENING GOVERNANCE, QUALITY AND RESPONSIBLE VALUE CREATION
Product leadership at Premier Energies extends beyond manufacturing scale. It is supported by responsible sourcing, product quality, regulatory compliance, customer focus, ethical business practices and disciplined financial governance.
Solar modules and solar cells remained the core of the 
Company’s business, contributing 77.19% and 20.15% of FY 2026 turnover, respectively.These efforts support local economic development and supply chain resilience, while the Company continues to strengthen governance through quality management, regulatory compliance, responsible manufacturing and transparent stakeholder engagement.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION & ANALYSIS
INDIAN ECONOMY
India’s economy demonstrated resilience and sustained 
momentum during FY 2026, supported by robust domestic demand, sustained public capital expenditure and a broad-based recovery across manufacturing and services. Despite global geopolitical and trade uncertainties, strong macroeconomic fundamentals and continued structural reforms supported economic growth.
Real GDP growth accelerated to 7.7% in FY 2026 from 7.1% 
in FY 2025, following the rebasing of national accounts to FY 2023. Nominal GDP grew 8.9% to INR 346.36 trillion, reaffirming India’s position as the fastest-growing major economy, according to the World Bank’s April 2026 Outlook.
Economic activity remained broad-based across key sectors, 
with services continuing to be the primary growth engine. Services GVA grew 8.1%, while construction, manufacturing, utilities and agriculture recorded growth of 9.4%, 4.5%, 5.9% and 4.6%, respectively. Sustained investment activity across infrastructure, manufacturing, energy transition and digital infrastructure, continued to support economic expansion.
Inflation remained subdued in FY 2026, with average CPI 
inflation easing to approximately 2.1%, from 4.6% in FY 2025. CPI inflation fell to a record low of 0.25% in October 2025, supported by moderating food prices and GST rate rationalisation, before rising to 3.4% in March 2026, while remaining within the RBI’s 2-6% tolerance band. GDP Growth Trend
Source: NSO, MoSPIFinancial environment remained supportive of economic activity, with non-food bank credit growing 15.9% YoY to INR 212.9 trillion, led by lending to services, personal loans, agriculture and industry. Banking-sector asset quality also improved, with Gross NPAs declining to 1.8% and Net NPAs to 0.4% as of March 2026, supporting the investment cycle.
Outlook
India’s growth outlook remains resilient despite global headwinds, supported by strong domestic demand, investment, manufacturing activity and policy support. As of August 2026, the RBI projects 6.7% GDP growth in FY 2027. CPI inflation is projected at 5.0%, reflecting potential pressures from energy prices, supply-chains disruptions and monsoon uncertainties. India’s medium-term agenda remains focussed on investment, manufacturing and energy transition, including setting up 500 GW of non-fossil fuel power generation capacity by 2030, supporting a favourable outlook for the domestic renewable energy sector.Gross NPA and Net NPA for Scheduled Commercial Banks

CORPORATE OVERVIEW FINANCIAL STATEMENTS STATUTORY REPORTSRenewable Energy Sector Overview – Global
Global renewable energy deployment accelerated in 2025, with 
renewable capacity additions reaching a record 800 GW, up 16% YoY. Solar PV accounted for 605 GW, nearly three-fourths of global additions, while wind contributed 159 GW. Other renewable technologies, including hydropower, bioenergy, geothermal and marine energy, contributed approximately  
33 GW.
The strong capacity build-out translated into higher renewable 
generation. Solar PV generation increased by a record 600 TWh in 2025, the largest annual increase recorded for any electricity source outside periods of post-crisis recovery, reaching nearly 2,700 TWh and taking solar’s share of global electricity generation to more than 8%. Total renewable generation grew by approximately 8.5% YoY, taking renewables’ share of global electricity generation to 34%, up from 32% in 2024. 
The acceleration in renewable deployment is driven by a 
structural shift towards greater electrification. Global energy demand growth accelerated to 2.0% in 2024, well above the 1.3% average recorded during 2013-2023, before moderating to 1.3% in 2025. Electricity demand, continued to grow faster supported by the electrification of transport, industry and buildings, alongside rapid digitalisation and data-centre expansion. In 2025, global electricity demand increased by around 3%, approximately 2.3 times the growth in overall energy demand, further expanding the addressable market for power generation.

Solar PV
Globally, data centre electricity consumption grew by around 
17% in 2025, with the rapid expansion of AI infrastructure emerging as a major driver of new electricity demand. In the United States, data centres accounted for approximately 50% of total electricity demand growth during the year, while the IEA projects their electricity consumption to more than double by 2030. Given their large, continuous and highly creditworthy power requirements, data centres are increasingly becoming key offtakers for long-term renewable power purchase agreements and co-located solar-plus-storage capacity.
Renewables are increasingly central to meeting rising power 
demand. Low-emissions sources met nearly 60% of global energy demand growth, with solar PV alone accounting for more than 25% of incremental demand, making it the largest single contributor. Natural gas, oil, solid bioenergy and waste, wind and coal contributed 17%, 15%, 13%, 9% and 9%, respectively. 
=== page 23 ===
Annual Report
2025-2638
This transition is also reshaping the global power mix. Renewable 
power generation now nearly matches coal-fired generation globally, while coal-fired electricity generation declined in China and India for the first time in five decades, despite increasing in the United States. Meanwhile, more than 80 GW of coal capacity was completed in 2025, largely reflecting projects permitted in 2022-23. In contrast, cumulative solar capacity crossed 2,800 GW, making solar the world’s largest installed generation technology.
Growth outlook
As per BloombergNEF’s New Energy Outlook 2026, global electricity demand is projected to increase by 69% by 2050, driven by economic and demographic growth, widespread electrification, higher cooling requirements and rapid expansion of data centres. Electricity demand is expected to rise 29% by 2035 alone, with developing economies continuing to account for a significant share of growth. BloombergNEF further estimates that annual energy-transition investment will average approximately USD 3.1 trillion between 2026 and 2030, 34% above 2025 levels, with investment required across renewable energy, power grids, battery storage and other enabling infrastructure to support the increasingly electrified energy system.
Solar and wind power are expected to remain at the forefront 
of this transition, their combined share of global electricity generation is projected to increase from 18% in 2025 to 56% by 2050, while the share of fossil fuels declines from 56% to 26%. Continued cost reductions and rising electricity demand are expected to further accelerate the shift towards renewable power.The evolving global energy landscape is reshaping solar manufacturing, with localisation, technology advancement and supply-chain concentration emerging as key industry dynamics. China accounts for around 85% of global solar PV manufacturing capacity and 95% of wafer production, making supply-chain diversification a strategic priority for key markets globally. The United States, European Union, India and Southeast Asia are increasingly promoting domestic manufacturing through tariffs, content requirements and production incentives.
With global solar module manufacturing capacity exceeding 1,500 
GW annually against global installations of 605 GW in 2025, the industry remains structurally oversupplied, shifting the competitive battleground from capacity expansion towards cost position, cell-level technology and backward integration. As per BloombergNEF’s Energy Transition Supply Chains 2026, India is emerging as the world’s second-largest module manufacturing base. Rapid expansion of its cell manufacturing capabilities positions it among the key beneficiaries of the global diversification of solar  
supply chains.
Renewable Energy Sector Overview – India
FY 2026 marked a significant inflection point in India’s energy transition, as three key structural milestones converged. First, scale: total installed power capacity crossed approximately 533 GW, with non-fossil sources accounting for 283.46 GW (approximately 53%), comprising 274.68 GW of renewables and 8.78 GW of nuclear capacity. Second, pace: non-fossil capacity additions in FY 2026 reached 55.29 GW, nearly double the previous record of 29.5 GW set a year earlier, led by 44.61 GW of solar additions. Third, commitment: India crossed the 50% non-fossil capacity milestone in June 2025, five years ahead of its 2030 Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC) target, prompting the Union Cabinet in March 2026 to raise the ambition to a 60% non-fossil share by 2035 under the updated NDC (2031-2035), alongside a 47% reduction in GDP emissions intensity from 2005 levels. India now ranks third globally in installed renewable energy capacity, having overtaken Brazil, according to IRENA’s Renewable Energy Statistics 2026 (Source: PIB/MNRE, 10 April 2026; PIB Cabinet release, 25 March 2026; CEA)

Distributed renewable energy emerged as a significant 
component of the year’s growth, contributing 16.31 GW, approximately 36% of solar additions, including 7.67 GW under PM-KUSUM and 8.71 GW of rooftop solar, of which 6.72 GW was attributable to PMSGY. During 2025, India also overtook the United States to become the world’s second-largest solar market by annual additions, behind only China.
Manufacturing has emerged as a key engine of India’s 
energy transition, with increasing emphasis on domestic and upstream integration. As per Mercom India, as of 31 March 2026, India had approximately 172 GW of solar module manufacturing capacity (from 2.3 GW in 2014) and 31 GW of operational solar cell manufacturing capacity. Cell capacity continues to trail behind module capacity due to higher capital intensity and more complex technology, making upstream cell manufacturing a key opportunity for domestic value addition and pricing power. Domestic manufacturers exported approximately 5 GW of solar modules during 2025, with the United States accounting for 96.8% of module exports, underscoring the country’s importance as a key export market for Indian manufacturers. However, high dependence on the US also exposed exporters to evolving trade and tariff policies. In February 2026, the US announced a preliminary countervailing duty of 125.87% on Indian crystalline silicon photovoltaic cells, while concurrent anti-dumping investigations added further uncertainty to market access. 
As per CEA, coal-fired generation in India declined 
by 3.8% in FY 2026, supported by rapid renewable capacity additions and favourable monsoon conditions. Renewables, including large hydro, accounted for 25.62% of total generation, while thermal generation accounted for 71.36%. Solar contributed 9.46% of national generation, followed by wind at 5.57%. Solar generation grew by 20.2%, while wind generation increased by 22.3% during the year. With the growing share of variable renewable generation - grid integration, storage deployment and transmission availability are emerging as key near-term constraints for the sector.

CORPORATE OVERVIEW FINANCIAL STATEMENTS STATUTORY REPORTSGoing forward, three structural demand engines are 
increasingly shaping India’s energy transition. First, end-use electrification is accelerating across transport, industry and buildings. India’s broad-gauge railway network was 99.6% electrified as of July 2026, while the IEA identifies rising EV adoption, industrial activity and cooling needs as key drivers of electricity demand. Green hydrogen is emerging as another electricity-intensive application, with electrolysis-based production requiring substantial amounts of renewable electricity. India’s National Green Hydrogen Mission targets 5 MMT of annual green hydrogen production by 2032, supported by about 125 GW of associated renewable energy capacity.
Second, digital infrastructure: rapid growth in AI-enabled 
computing is making data centres an increasingly important source of electricity demand. In India, data-centre capacity reached approximately 1.9 GW in FY 2026, with approximately 4.5 GW development pipeline, creating approximately 24 GW of potential solar demand by 2030. 
Growth is also being supported by state-level data-centre 
policies and investment incentives. Gujarat’s Viksit Gujarat Data Centre Policy 2026-29 targets 7.5 GW data-centre capacity and INR 6 trillion of investment, with a requirement for at least 51% of electricity supply coming from green and renewable sources. Rajasthan’s Data Centre Policy 2025 offers fiscal and operational incentives, including support for captive renewable energy, while Andhra Pradesh’s Data Centre Policy 2024-29 targets 200 MW additional capacity and provides incentives for large-scale and AI-enabled data-centre projects. Together, rising data-centre loads and policy-led renewable requirements are creating a significant opportunity for solar, storage and power-infrastructure providers.
Third, energy security: geopolitical volatility and fossil-fuel price 
uncertainty have strengthened the role of renewables as an energy-security solution alongside decarbonisation, with solar and wind reducing exposure to global commodity price cycles. For fuel-importing economies such as India, domestically generated renewable power can therefore support both energy independence and long-term cost stability.Source-wise Net Capacity Addition (GW)

Policy support
India’s solar growth is supported by a robust policy framework alongside strong underlying demand drivers. Policy measures are designed to address both the demand and supply sides.
FY 2026 marked a shift in policy support, with two 
key developments. First, policy focus broadened from capacity addition to system integration, encompassing grid management, energy storage and agricultural solarisation, alongside private-sector innovation in digital grid management, forecasting generation output and time-of-use tariffs. Second, and particularly relevant for manufacturers, policy increasingly focused on market access, with ALMM mandates, DCR requirements and duty structures shaping eligibility and competitiveness. These measures favour players with greater domestic integration and in-house cell manufacturing capabilities. The sector’s economic competitiveness continues to reinforce demand, with solar tariffs remaining below those of new coal-fired generation. Budget 2026-2027 further supported the ecosystem through approximately INR 10,000 million of viability gap funding for BESS and pumped storage projects, INR 1,80,000 million for the Revamped Distribution Sector Scheme and customs duty relief for clean-energy manufacturing inputs.  
Battery Energy Storage System Demand
Battery energy storage has evolved from an optional add-on to a key requirement for renewable energy integration. In 2025, BESS was the world’s fastest-growing power technology, with installations rising approximately 40% to nearly 110 GW. BloombergNEF projects global stationary storage capacity to increase from approximately 223 GW in 2025 to nearly 3.8 TW by 2050. This shift is accelerating the move towards firm and dispatchable renewable power, including solar-plus-storage, round-the-clock and hybrid projects.In India, the rapid expansion of renewable capacity is placing 
increasing pressure on grid integration and transmission infrastructure. Approximately 470 GWh of renewable generation was curtailed across inter-state projects in Q4 FY 2026, of which approximately 300 GWh was attributed to transmission constraints, particularly in the Northern and Western grids. Renewable-rich states such as Rajasthan and Gujarat face the most acute evacuation bottlenecks. Ember report 2025 estimates that 3-4 GW of two-hour battery storage could absorb much of the currently curtailed generation.  India has missed its annual transmission targets by about 20% over the past five years, while one in four major transmission schemes is delayed by at least a year. This is expected to drive INR 5-6 trillion of new transmission capital expenditure between FY 2026-27 and FY 2031-32, with approximately 107 GW of inter-state connectivity already granted through FY 2030-31.
BESS can store surplus solar and wind generation for 
periods of low output, thereby improving grid stability, energy security and renewable utilisation. Government support is accelerating deployment, including the Ministry of Power’s additional VGF scheme for 30 GWh of BESS with INR 54,000 million outlay, following the earlier 13.2 GWh scheme. The Electricity (Amendment) Rules, 2025 have also clarified the ownership, deployment and legal status of energy storage systems, enabling standalone and co-located deployment as well as leasing and sale of storage capacity. 
The National Electricity Plan estimates India’s total energy 
storage requirement at approximately 411 GWh by FY 2031-32, comprising 236 GWh of BESS and 175 GWh of pumped storage. (Source: CEA, National Electricity Plan). With the levelised cost of storage at approximately INR 4.0-4.5/kWh and solar at approximately INR 2.5/kWh, solar-plus-storage

costs in several states. Alongside VGF-backed projects, growing storage and hybrid tenders are accelerating demand for storage-integrated and grid-forming solutions.
While utility-scale projects currently dominate deployment, 
residential and behind-the-meter storage is emerging as a distinct growth segment, supported by rooftop solar adoption under PMSGY, net-metering and the need for reliable backup power. India’s BESS market across utility, C&I and residential segments could exceed 160 GWh by 2030, including an estimated 6-8 GWh of residential demand. 
Transformers 
India’s transformer market is entering a strong growth phase, supported by rising electricity demand, renewable-capacity additions and the resulting need for grid infrastructure upgrades. As per Deloitte’s 2025 industry assessment, India’s electricity demand is growing at a CAGR of 5.5% and is expected to double over the next 10-12 years. Generation capacity is projected to grow by 40-50 GW annually, largely led by renewables, driving demand for Inverter Duty (IDT), High Voltage (HV) and Extra High Voltage (EHV) transformers for reliable grid integration and transmission stability.
Beyond the power sector, emerging applications such as data 
centres, metro rail and railway infrastructure are expected to provide additional sources of transformer demand, with 5 GW of data-centre capacity and 1,900 km of metro lines targeted by 2030. As per the National Electricity Plan (NEP), transformation capacity is expected to increase from 1,251 GVA to 2,342 GVA, while the transmission network is projected to expand from 4.85 lakh circuit km to 6.48 lakh circuit km.
Generation and transmission-side power-transformer 
demand is estimated at 170 GVA in FY 2026, increasing to 280 GVA by FY 2030, implying a 13% CAGR. The industry has approximately 250 GVA of effective annual production capacity against 280 GVA of FY 2027E demand, with capacity constraints and raw-material shortages contributing to lead times of up to 18-24 months in HV and EHV segments.
Growth outlook
India’s solar sector is positioned for continued expansion, with approximately 48-50 GW of new solar capacity expected to be added in FY 2027. The medium-term outlook is supported by a substantial renewable-energy project pipeline and long-term capacity targets. As of March 2026, 138.32 GW of renewable-energy capacity was under construction, comprising 90.04 GW of solar, 29.28 GW of wind and 19.00 GW of hybrid projects according to the Central Electricity Authority (CEA). The CEA’s National Electricity Plan projects India’s installed generating capacity to reach approximately 900 GW by 2031-32, including 364.6 GW of solar capacity, 2.4 times the March 2026 installed solar base of 150.26 GW, implying an indicative CAGR of 15.9%.
Accelerating domestic demand, supportive policies, 
manufacturing incentives, storage integration and global supply-chain diversification are creating a favourable environment for India’s solar manufacturing industry. As the market shifts towards higher-efficiency technologies and greater localisation, integrated manufacturers are well positioned to capture opportunities across domestic and international markets. 
Company overview 
With over three decades of experience in the photovoltaic industry, Premier Energies has established itself as one of India’s leading integrated solar manufacturers. Headquartered in Hyderabad, Telangana. The Company is advancing towards its vision of becoming an integrated clean technology solutions provider, with capabilities spanning ingots, wafers, solar cells and modules, alongside an expanding portfolio across BESS, power transformers, aluminium frames and EPC services.
During FY 2026, Premier Energies scaled up its manufacturing 
footprint, with solar cell capacity reaching 3.6 GW and module capacity 11.1 GW. The year witnessed the commissioning of a 1.4 GW TOPCon module facility in Hyderabad, Telangana in May 2025 and a 1.2 GW TOPCon cell facility in July 2025, followed by 400 MW of brownfield cell and 350 MW of brownfield module expansion in January 2026. A key milestone during the year was the commissioning of the 5.6 GW module manufacturing facility at Seetharampur, Telangana. Equipped with fully automated production lines capable of producing four modules every 16 seconds, the facility integrates TOPCon technology with Zero Busbar architecture and AI-enabled real-time defect detection for enhanced quality control. The facility has created more than 2,000 jobs, contributing to local economic development. 
Under Mission 2028, Premier Energies is scaling its vertically 
integrated manufacturing ecosystem through focused investments across Telangana and Andhra Pradesh. The Naidupeta facility in Andhra Pradesh, comprising 7 GW TOPCon solar cell capacity and 10 GW ingot-wafer capacity, is at the heart of this expansion. At Seetharampur, Telangana, construction is underway for the 12 GWh BESS and 18,000 MT aluminium frame manufacturing facilities, with completion targeted as outlined below.

Product portfolio 
In FY 2026, Premier Energies’ revenue was primarily driven by solar cells, solar modules and EPC services.
Revenue Source
I. Solar Cells
Premier Energies manufactures P-type Mono PERC and N-type TOPCon photovoltaic cells including M10R 10BB Mono PERC solar cell, G12R 16BB N-Type Bifacial solar cell and G12R 0BB N-Type Bifacial solar cell. 
M10R 10BB Mono PERC Bifacial Solar Cell 
The M10R Mono PERC solar cell measures 182.2 mm × 
183.75 mm and has an average conversion efficiency of 23.5%. It features an anisotropically etched surface, a half-cut design, anti-PID and LID performance and a narrow rear-busbar design to reduce shading and improve bifacial performance.
    
G12R 16BB N-Type Bifacial Solar Cell 
The G12R 16BB solar cell measures 182.3 mm × 210 mm and is based on N-type technology. It has an average conversion efficiency of 25.5% at mass-production scale, with a low temperature coefficient of -0.29%/°C and bifaciality of 80 ± 5%. 
G12R 0BB N-Type Bifacial Solar Cell 
The G12R 0BB solar cell measures 182.3 mm × 210 mm and is based on N-type technology. It targets an average conversion efficiency of 25.8% at mass-production scale, with a low temperature coefficient of -0.29%/°C and bifaciality of 80 ± 5%. II. Solar ModulesPremier Energies manufactures a range of P-type Mono PERC and N-type TOPCon bifacial solar modules across multiple glass backsheet configurations using M10, M10R and G12R cells with power outputs of up to 630 W. All modules carry a 30-year linear power warranty and a 12-year product warranty.
 P-Type M10 HC Mono PERC Bifacial Transparent 
Back Sheet Module 
This module offers bifaciality of up to 70 ± 10% and is available in 380-410 W, 420-450 W and 525-555 W power ranges.
 P-Type M10 Mono PERC Bifacial Dual Glass Module 
This module delivers up to 30% additional power generation compared with conventional P-type modules and is available in 380-555 W sizes.
N-Type M10R & G12R Bifacial Dual Glass Module 
The N-type bifacial dual-glass module is available with M10R and G12R cells. The M10R configuration offers 570-590 W, while the G12R configuration offers 600-630 W. The modules offer up to 30% additional power generation compared with conventional P-type modules.
 N-Type G12R HC Bifacial Dual Glass Module Neo Black 
This module delivers 600-630 W with up to 30% additional power generation compared with conventional P-type modules alongside enhanced aesthetics. 
III. EPC Solutions Premier Energies has over 12 years of solar EPC experience, delivering turnkey solutions across ground-mounted, rooftop, floating, canal-top and hybrid projects. The 51% stake in HeliosAnthos Energies Private Limited enhances the Company’s project execution capabilities across wind and BESS projects. 
IV. Operations and Maintenance (O&M) Solutions Premier Energies provides operations and maintenance solutions to its EPC customers across ground-mounted, rooftop solar and solar water-pump installations. 
V. Transformers 
Transcon offers a comprehensive portfolio of power solutions spanning inverter duty, power, distribution, LV dry-type, compact substation and BESS transformers. Its product range extends up to 765 kV, supported by in-house engineering and advanced manufacturing capabilities. The business also offers customised solutions for solar and storage applications, enabling Premier Energies to address a broader range of customer requirements across the renewable energy value chain.

CORPORATE OVERVIEW FINANCIAL STATEMENTS STATUTORY REPORTSRisk Management 
The renewable energy industry is undergoing rapid capacity 
expansion, technology change and supply-chain realignment, while the Company is scaling its manufacturing capacity and expanding into adjacent businesses. These developments present significant opportunities for growth, while also introducing several operational and financial risks. Premier Energies follows an enterprise-wide risk management framework to identify, assess and mitigate material risks through defined ownership, operating controls and periodic management review. The framework is designed to support business continuity, protect capital and assets and enable the Company’s growth plans while maintaining financial and operational resilience. 
Risk Area Risk Overview Potential Impact Mitigation Strategy
Production Risk Rapid scaling of manufacturing 
across multiple facilities increases exposure to process instability and ramp-up challenges at newly commissioned linesLow-capacity utilisation, production losses, delivery delays and high operating costsOperational resilience is being strengthened through standardised processes, automation, real-time monitoring, preventive maintenance and robust quality controls, supported by phased commissioning, in-house engineering capabilities and experienced operating teams
Supply Chain Risk Dependence on imported raw materials, components and manufacturing equipment exposes the Company to price volatility, logistic disruptions, trade restrictions and geopolitical developments Input-cost inflation, procurement delays and production disruption resulting in margin pressureSupplier diversification, focus on long-term sourcing contracts, increased domestic sourcing and backward integration 
Technology Obsolescence Rapid evolution in cell and module technologies may reduce the competitiveness of existing manufacturing facilities and product rangePoor customer acceptance, reduced product competitiveness and higher technology-related capital expenditure requirementsScaling of high-efficiency TOPCon and Zero Busbar technologies, technology tie-ups with RENA, Germany for process innovation in N-Type and next-generation tandem technologies and R&D collaboration 
Industry OvercapacityModule oversupply and aggressive pricing Lower price realisations, margin compression and reduced profitabilityUpstream integration diversification across market segments including transformers and BESS focus on higher-value DCR-modules, differentiated offerings including Zero Busbar and All-Black modules to maintain industry leadership and competitiveness
Foreign Exchange Volatility Lower revenues and / or higher costs to currency fluctuationsEarnings volatility, higher input costs and reduced export competitivenessEffective currency hedging, minimal export exposure, sharing risk with consumers
Project Execution Risk Large-scale investments in manufacturing capacity and entry into adjacent businesses involve high execution, commissioning and technology risks Project delays, cost overruns, delayed ramp-up and lower-than-expected returns on invested capitalPartnership with experienced technology providers, milestone-based monitoring, comprehensive project insurances
New Business Risk Expansion into new businesses introduces new product, technology, execution and market risks Slower-than-planned commercialisation, execution challenges and lower returns from new investmentsLow upfront capital expenditure, leveraging existing capabilities and relationships with respective market leaders
Delay in customer paymentsExposure to EPC contractors, developers, distributors and institutional customers creates counterparty and collection risksHigher receivables, increased working capital requirements and potential credit lossesDiversified customer base, advance-payment mechanisms, letters of credit and defined credit limits

Technology and Product Innovation 
The solar industry is rapidly transitioning towards higher-
efficiency cell technologies to improve energy yields, reduce degradation and enhance cost competitiveness. TOPCon has emerged as a leading mainstream architecture, offering higher conversion efficiency and greater bifaciality, while innovations such as Zero Busbar, advanced metallisation and larger wafer formats are enabling higher power density and manufacturing efficiency.
Against this backdrop, Premier Energies is advancing 
its technology leadership through scale-up of TOPCon manufacturing, product innovation and continuous R&D. During the year, the Company achieved TOPCon cell efficiencies of up to 25.5% and introduced the NeoBlack Series, an all-black G12R DCR TOPCon module rated at up to 630 Wp, strengthening its portfolio of high-performance solar solutions.
Premier Energies is also building capabilities in next-
generation cell architectures including tandem technologies. Its collaboration with RENA Technologies GmbH, Germany, focuses on advanced wet-chemical processes for N-type, TOPCon and silicon-perovskite tandem technologies, complemented by its R&D collaboration with BITS Pilani, Hyderabad. Advanced automation, AI-enabled defect detection and process optimisation further support manufacturing quality and efficiency.
The Company is also strengthening its technology and 
engineering capabilities in power infrastructure through Transcon. Its technology platform incorporates in-house amorphous metal core manufacturing, vacuum drying, precision foil winding and impulse and partial discharge testing, supported by in-house engineering for customised power solutions. These capabilities, together with compliance with global standards including UL, ANSI, IEEE and IEC, support the delivery of reliable and application-specific transformer solutions. These initiatives support Premier Energies’ evolution towards a broader, technology-led clean energy platform.
People 
Premier Energies’ growth and expansion during FY 2026 were supported by a continued focus on building a skilled, agile and future-ready workforce. As the Company ramped up new manufacturing facilities and expanded its R&D capabilities, it strengthened talent acquisition, skill training and leadership development initiatives across the organisation. The introduction of PMS 2.0 established a more structured performance-management framework, aligning individual objectives with business priorities through measurable KRAs, biannual reviews and digitised, evidence-based assessments. The CXO Leadership Programme further supported the development of high-potential talent and strengthened the Company’s leadership pipeline. 
Learning and development remained a key priority during 
the year, with more than 40,000 hours of employee training undertaken across the organisation, supporting technical, functional and future-oriented capabilities. The Company continued to expand employment opportunities associated with the clean energy transition, supporting 9,220 green jobs during FY 2026. The Company also established a Skill Development Centre at Naidupeta to equip local youth and women with industry-relevant skills and strengthen the local talent ecosystem. 
Premier Energies is committed to fostering a workplace built on 
diversity, equal opportunity and employee wellbeing. Women accounted for 25% of the Board and 30% of the shopfloor workforce. The Company continues to promote women’s participation in manufacturing, supported by maternity benefits, nursing and childcare facilities and grievance redressal mechanisms, while remaining committed to accessibility and non-discrimination for persons with disabilities. Reflecting the strength of its people practices and workplace culture, Premier Energies was recognised as a Great Place to Work for the fifth consecutive year.
Employee health and safety remain core priorities, 
supported by an ISO 45001:2018-certified Occupational Health and Safety Management System, hazard and risk assessments, safety training and regular monitoring. In FY 2026, the Company recorded zero fatalities and zero Lost Time Injury Frequency Rate. Employee wellbeing is supported through regular health camps, medical support, insurance coverage, occupational health centres and affordable rental housing near manufacturing facilities. Rewards and recognition programmes, including Stars of the Month and Teams of the Month, along with ESOP participation and comprehensive social security benefits reinforce a culture of recognition, inclusion and shared success.
As Premier Energies enters its next phase of expansion, 
it remains focused on building a future-ready workforce, strengthening leadership capabilities and fostering an inclusive environment that enables employees to perform, develop and thrive.

Community engagement remained integral to the

Internal Control System 
Premier Energies Limited maintains robust systems and processes to support operational efficiency, compliance and reliable financial reporting with regular internal audits and adherence to policies and legal requirements. The Company leverages SAP HANA, HRONE, ADP and in-house business-control platforms including Zion, LEAF, CertifyPro, CCAS and SPOT to strengthen controls across HR, procurement, manufacturing, sales and finance. These systems are continuously enhanced based on audit findings and business requirements with measures in place to safeguard financial reporting and mitigate fraud risk. No internal audit findings or material remediation actions have been reported in relation to these controls. The Audit Committee oversees the effectiveness of the internal control and risk-management framework including risks arising from human error and high-risk areas such as anti-corruption.

1.2 Operational Performance
In FY 2026, Premier Energies strengthened its position 
as one of India’s leading integrated solar photovoltaic module manufacturers helped by sustained operational excellence, capacity expansion, technology innovation and growing demand for solar products. A diversified customer base and focus on manufacturing efficiency enabled the Company to deliver strong operational and financial performance.
A key milestone during the year was the commissioning 
of the 5.6 GW Solar Photovoltaic TOPCon module manufacturing facility at Seetharampur, Telangana. The Company’s aggregate annual module manufacturing capacity now stands at 11.1 GW, significantly enhancing its ability to serve the growing demand for high-efficiency solar modules. The highly automated facility incorporates advanced manufacturing technologies, including AI-enabled quality control systems and Zero Busbar -compatible production capabilities, supporting consistent product quality, reliability and manufacturing efficiency.The Company also advanced its technology and product portfolio through the introduction of next-generation solar solutions. During the year, it launched India’s first Zero Busbar (0BB) TOPCon Solar Cell, designed to improve cell efficiency, reduce silver consumption and enhance module reliability. It has also recently introduced India’s first All-Black G12R DCR TOPCon solar module, a premium product for residential and commercial rooftop applications with high standards of performance and durability.
Premier Energies’ manufacturing and product capabilities 
received global recognition during the year, with the Company ranked among the Top 25 Global and Top 3 Indian Grade A Solar PV Module Manufacturers in the Wood Mackenzie Global Solar PV Module Manufacturer Ranking 2026. The recognition reflects the Company’s advanced manufacturing capabilities, technological maturity, financial strength, supply chain resilience and focus on quality and sustainability.
=== page 39 ===
Annual Report
2025-2654
The Company is constantly striving to build a more 
integrated domestic solar manufacturing ecosystem through investments in capacity, technology and vertical integration. These initiatives are expected to enhance supply chain resilience, strengthen competitiveness and position the Company to meet growing demand for high-efficiency solar products across domestic and international markets.With its continued focus on innovation, manufacturing excellence, customer-centricity and sustainable growth, the Company remains well positioned to capture opportunities arising from India’s accelerating renewable energy transition and the growing emphasis on domestic manufacturing.

11. Internal Financial Control and its Adequacy
The Company has instituted an adequate and effective Internal Financial Controls (IFC) system over financial reporting, designed to provide reasonable assurance regarding the accuracy, reliability, and timeliness of financial disclosures. These controls ensure that all transactions are appropriately authorised, recorded, and reported in accordance with applicable regulatory and internal policy requirements.
To support operational governance and financial discipline, 
the Company has established comprehensive Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs), policies, and an Authority/Commercial Manual. These documents guide the conduct of business across functions and help maintain compliance with legal and regulatory obligations. Responsibility for ensuring adherence to these frameworks rests with the respective functional heads.
The Company continues to leverage SAP S/4HANA 
as its ERP platform, with the objective of continuously strengthening its internal financial control environment. Enhancements or upgrades to the SAP S/4HANA system are considered as and when required to align with evolving business needs and compliance requirements.
All material changes in accounting policies and their 
impact on financial statements are subject to validation by the Statutory Auditors and review by the Audit Committee prior to implementation and disclosure.

28. Human Resources Framework
The Company recognizes its employees as a critical enabler of sustained business performance and long-term value creation. The Human Resources strategy for FY 2025-26 aligned with this principle, with a focused emphasis on building a future-ready, high-performance, and agile workforce capable of supporting the Company’s strategic growth objectives.During the year, the Company has further strengthened its people practices through an integrated HR framework covering talent management, performance governance, capability building, and employee experience. A structured talent management approach continues to enable identification of critical roles, strengthening of internal talent pipelines, and succession planning across key positions. Leadership development initiatives for CXO and senior leadership cohorts have been enhanced to build strategic capability, execution excellence, and long-term leadership depth.
A key development during the year has been the 
strengthening of the performance management system through PMS 2.0, designed to institutionalize a more structured and outcome-driven approach to performance governance. The framework emphasizes alignment of individual goals with enterprise priorities, measurable KRAs, biannual performance checkpoints, and greater reliance on evidence-based evaluation supported through digitized HR systems. 
Capability building continues to remain a key focus area, 
with targeted interventions across functional excellence, leadership development, and future-ready skills such as automation, digital transformation, sustainability (ESG), and advanced manufacturing practices. These initiatives are supported through structured learning pathways and strategic external partnerships aimed at strengthening organizational capability and fostering innovation.
Employee engagement and well-being remain central to the 
Company’s people agenda. The organization continues to invest in structured engagement frameworks, recognition programs, and employee wellness initiatives, supported by enhanced health coverage, occupational health support systems, and improved workplace infrastructure across locations.
The Company continues to maintain a strong focus 
on occupational health and safety, reinforced through ISO 45001 certification and a robust safety governance framework. This includes contractor safety compliance, incident management systems, and preventive safety practices to ensure a safe and disciplined working environment across all operations.
Diversity and inclusion remain an integral part of the 
organization’s people strategy, with more than 30% diversity,  continued efforts to strengthen workforce diversity and foster an inclusive workplace that enables equal opportunity and growth. The Company has also maintained harmonious industrial relations and ensured full 
=== page 46 ===

29.  Compliance with the Maternity Benefit  
Act, 1961
Premier Energies is dedicated to upholding the rights and welfare of its female employees by ensuring full compliance with the Maternity Benefit Act, 1961. Key compliance measures include:
	•Providing maternity leave and benefits as stipulated under the Act.
	•Ensuring job protection and non-discrimination during and after maternity leave.
	•Facilitating appropriate workplace accommodations such as nursing rooms and creche facilities to support new mothers.
	•Offering nursing breaks and other maternity-related support to promote employee well-being.
	•Communicating maternity policies clearly to all employees and maintaining transparent grievance redressal mechanisms.
These initiatives reflect our commitment to supporting women employees throughout their maternity journey and fostering an inclusive and supportive workplace.

32. Capital Expenditure Programs 
During the financial year 2025–26, Premier Energies Limited, through its wholly owned subsidiary Premier Energies Global Environment Private Limited (PEGEPL), initiated significant capital expenditure programs aimed at enhancing its manufacturing capabilities and achieving full vertical integration across the solar value chain. These initiatives are strategically aligned with the Company’s long-term vision of becoming a globally competitive, fully integrated solar energy solutions provider.
Key capital expenditure highlights include:
	•Acquisition of 55 Acres at Seetharampur, Ranga 
Reddy District, Telangana: PEGEPL acquired 55 acres of land from Telangana State Industrial Infrastructure Corporation (TSIIC), located at the Industrial Park at Industrial Park, Seetharampur, Ranga Reddy District, Telangana. This site is earmarked for the establishment of a 6 GWh Battery Energy Storage Solution (BESS) and 18,000 MT of Aluminium manufacturing facility.
	•Acquisition of 21 Acres at Fabcity, Telangana: The Company also acquired 21 acres of land from 
Telangana Industrial Infrastructure Corporation (TGIIC) at the Industrial Park in E-ctiy, Fabcity, Ranga Reddy District, Telangana for the development of a research & development and centre of excellence for future technologies. This facility will support the R&D and skill development for next generation technologies.
33. Technology Advancement
The Company continues to strengthen its technology leadership by accelerating the transition towards next-
=== page 47 ===
Annual Report
2025-2662
generation cell technologies. During the year, the Company 
commissioned its advanced 5.6 GW TOPCon solar module manufacturing facility, incorporating Zero Busbar (ZBB) technology to deliver higher efficiency products for both domestic and international markets. The Company is also progressing with its large-scale TOPCon solar cell manufacturing expansion at Naidupeta, which, upon completion, will significantly enhance its high-efficiency cell manufacturing capabilities. Leveraging the compatibility between PERC and TOPCon manufacturing infrastructure, the Company retains the flexibility to progressively migrate existing capacities in line with evolving market demand and technological advancements. Supported by continuous investments in research and development, including strategic collaborations with RENA Technologies (Germany) and BITS Pilani, Hyderabad, the Company remains well positioned to adopt future technologies and sustain its competitive advantage in an increasingly innovation-driven solar industry.

ANNUAL REPORT ON CSR ACTIVITIES
1. Brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company.
Premier Energies Limited is committed to integrating social responsibility into the core of its operations, aligning with the 
requirements set forth in Section 135 of the Companies Act, 2013. The Company actively seeks to uplift individuals and create avenues for their holistic development through a diverse range of initiatives. These initiatives are designed to enhance health, promote rural development, celebrate culture and heritage, and bolster education, among other vital efforts.
Our Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) policy establishes the foundational principles and strategic framework that guide 
the implementation of our CSR initiatives. Premier Energies Limited is dedicated to generating sustainable value for our key stakeholders.
Our CSR programs are thoughtfully crafted to be relevant in local, national, and global contexts, with a special focus on 
disadvantaged communities, and are grounded in universally accepted sustainable development principles. Through these efforts, we aim to make a meaningful impact and foster positive change in society.

(iii) Details related to Employee Stock Option Schemes 
PEL ESOP 2021 PEL ESOP Scheme 2025
The Board of Directors and the Shareholders of Premier Energies Limited, at their meetings held on September 4, 2021, and September 9, 2021, respectively, approved and adopted the Premier Energies Limited Employee Stock Option Scheme 2021 (“PEL ESOP 2021”). The objective of the scheme is to reward eligible employees through the grant of stock options which, upon exercise, result in the issuance of equivalent equity shares of the Company. This initiative is intended to recognize employee loyalty and their contributions toward sustained corporate growth and long-term value creation.
The Scheme became effective from September 9, 2021, 
and is administered by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee (“Committee”) through the PEL ESOP Trust (“Trust”) established by the Company for this purpose.The Board of Directors of the Company, based on the recommendation of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee, at its meeting held on February 3, 2025, approved the Premier Energies Limited Employee Stock Option Scheme, 2025 (“PEL ESOP Scheme 2025”). The Scheme was subsequently approved by the Members of the Company through a Postal Ballot dated April 6, 2025.
PEL ESOP Scheme 2025 has been formulated in 
accordance with the applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and the SEBI (Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021 (“SEBI SBEB Regulations”) with the objective of promoting the long-term financial interest of the Company by providing Eligible Employees an opportunity to participate in the share capital of the Company, thereby attracting and retaining talent, motivating employees, creating a sense of ownership and aligning employee interests with the long-term objectives and sustained growth of the Company.

2025-2684
PEL ESOP 2021 PEL ESOP Scheme 2025
The Scheme was subsequently amended pursuant to the 
resolutions passed by the Board of Directors at their meeting held on April 15, 2024, and by the Shareholders at their meeting held on April 18, 2024. The scheme remains fully compliant with the provisions of the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021 (“SEBI SBEB Regulations”).
Further, in compliance with Regulation 12 of the SEBI SBEB 
Regulations, the Company ratified the PEL ESOP 2021 pursuant to a resolution passed through a Postal Ballot dated April 6, 2025.

6.
Source of shares (primary, secondary or combination) The Trust acquires Shares by way of fresh allotment from the Company or secondary sales from existing shareholders. The Trust shall acquire or purchase Equity Shares of the Company by way of fresh allotment from the Company or through secondary acquisition from existing shareholders, or by way of both, as permitted under applicable laws and the provisions of PEL ESOP Scheme 2025.
7.
Variation in terms of options The Company may, by special resolution in a general meeting, vary the terms of PEL ESOP 2021 offered pursuant to an earlier resolution of a general body but not yet exercised by an Employee, provided such variation is not prejudicial to the interests of the Option holders.

ESOP 2021 in any manner, which may be detrimental to the interests of the Employees.No variation in the terms of PEL ESOP Scheme 2025 has been made during the financial year ended March 31, 2026.

1. COMPANY’S GOVERNANCE PHILOSOPHY
Premier Energies Limited views effective corporate 
governance as vital for sustainable growth, stakeholder confidence, and long-term value. For us, good governance goes beyond statutory compliance. It shapes how we make decisions and conduct business.
Our philosophy rests on integrity, accountability, 
transparency, fairness, and ethics. Guided by these principles, our Board and Management oversee operations through a robust framework of committees, policies, internal controls, and risk management processes. To maintain relevance and effectiveness, we continuously review these practices against evolving regulations and stakeholder expectations.CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT

Board Skills, Expertise and Competencies
The Board maintains an optimal mix of skills, expertise, and competencies aligned with the scale and nature of the Company’s operations. The core capabilities identified by the Board as essential for providing strategic direction and effective oversight are detailed below:
Sl. No Core Skills, Expertise and CompetenciesDescription

Board Process
The Board uses structured processes for effective oversight and informed decision-making. The Company Secretary circulates the agenda, explanatory notes, and supporting materials to Directors in advance. Urgent matters are taken up with the Chairman’s approval. The Company Secretary ensures compliance with the Companies Act, 2013, Listing Regulations, and other laws, while facilitating smooth Board and Committee operations. Committee minutes and recommendations are presented at subsequent Board meetings for noting or approval.
Information Placed Before the Board
The Board receives comprehensive information to ensure effective oversight of the Company’s affairs. This includes all disclosures required under Part A of Schedule II to the Listing Regulations. Specifically, the Board reviews financial and operational performance, budgets, capital expenditures, borrowings, risk management, internal controls, compliance, audit observations, related party transactions, CSR initiatives, and material litigations.
Remuneration of Directors
Director remuneration is recommended by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee, aligned with the Company’s Nomination and Remuneration Policy, and approved by the Board and shareholders where required. The remuneration payable to Non-Executive Directors is determined in accordance with the criteria set out in the Company’s Nomination and Remuneration Policy, which is available on the Company’s website at https://www.premierenergies.com/investor-relations .
Independent Directors receive sitting fees for attending Board and Committee meetings, alongside any applicable expense reimbursements. Other than these payments, Independent Directors maintained no pecuniary relationships or transactions with the Company during FY 2025-26.

statements and reporting processes before Board submission.

a. Financial Results
The quarterly, half-yearly and annual financial results of the Company, approved by the Board of Directors are submitted to the NSE and BSE in accordance with the requirements of the Listing Regulations. The financial results are published in Financial Express (English) and Nava Telangana (Telugu) and are also hosted on the Company’s website at www.premierenergies.com/investor-relations.
b. WebsiteThe Company maintains a dedicated Investor Relations section on its website,  
www.premierenergies.com/investor-relations,  which
